nurbspy / engineering performance study

One geometry. Two backends.

Numerical agreement, compilation cost and steady-state performance across basis functions, differential geometry, integration, construction, projection and complete workflows.

2026-10-27T18:48:23Z · CPU · float64 · Python 3.13.2 · source 4de05a0690

193workloads measured
182passed available checks
11require investigation
521backend measurements

Warm execution at a glance

Selected passing workloads. Each bar uses the median of synchronized calls; exact values and all workloads are below. Comparisons include Python dispatch.

NumPy · Numba disabledNumPy + NumbaJAX

What the checks found

Regular values, derivatives and geometric quantities should be assessed separately from convention differences and fixed-rule integration error. A REVIEW result is not automatically a defective algorithm.

On the 126 passing matched basis/curve/surface workloads, warm JAX is faster in 113 cases versus pure NumPy and 44 versus NumPy + Numba. Small calls can favor Numba. These are local descriptive counts, not a universal speedup; compilation and the number of repeated calls matter.

2D normal convention: NumPy get_normal is the principal Frenet normal; JAX get_normal uses a +90° rotated tangent. Their signs can differ. The separately measured NumPy get_normal_2D / rotated-JAX-tangent adapter checks the equivalent oriented-normal calculation.

Fixed during this study: NumPy/Numba basis derivatives now use an iterative recurrence, return zero above the degree, and retain lower-degree neighbors for non-clamped knots. This removes invalid indexing and the cached recursive calls that produced native process failures. Final timings use corrected source; before-fix evidence is retained. Regression tests cover independent SciPy results, scalars, arrays, complex-step inputs and disk-cache reuse across processes.

Length defaults are different algorithms: NumPy global Gauss-10 under-resolves the smooth and repeated-knot examples; JAX adaptive quadrature and the ODE pass the study threshold. Global JAX fixed Clenshaw-Curtis-41 also under-resolves these examples. Fixed-resolution rules need convergence checks or knot-span splitting; matching a tolerance argument is not equivalent work.

Collapsed-curve frames, curvature and torsion are undefined. The backends return different NaN/zero patterns. These differences require an explicit API policy, not a looser regular-geometry tolerance.

Surface area, whole-geometry energy workflows and NumPy finite-difference sensitivities are benchmark adapters rather than newly added production APIs. Offset surfaces use the package's sampled approximation, not exact geometric offsets.

Prepared tables and dense inverse maps amortize setup only when reused. The map-query row measures reuse separately from full inverse-map construction; table setup includes independent reference evaluation and is not a pure construction microbenchmark.

Projection limitation: NumPy/Numba's single local solve misses a strictly better feasible candidate in the 16-query curve batch; the squared-distance gap is 0.00123385. JAX's sampled initialization passes this batch. Tight optimizer tolerances do not ensure the correct basin; neither finite sampling nor multistart is a universal global certificate.

Inverse-map derivative accuracy: default unit-speed errors are 1.664e-04 (smooth) and 5.567e-04 (repeated knot). Using tol=1e-9 and 64 steps per span reduces them to 5.845e-07 and 1.857e-06. Parameter accuracy alone does not establish unit-speed accuracy. The existing tol=1e-6 is a local solver control, not a global derivative-error guarantee; no source default was changed.

Full public API coverage inventory · Documented production fixes

Full test suite: 507 passed, 6 skipped, 0 failed, 0 errors. Numerical references and saved-result integrity are checked separately by verify.py. With Numba JIT disabled: 66 additional focused/complex-step checks passed, 6 skipped. JUnit XML is retained locally under outputs/results/.

Times in milliseconds. Click an operation for tolerances, reference checks, setup time and samples. Click a column heading to sort. REVIEW can mean differing algorithms or accuracy, rather than a code defect.

How to interpret the study

Timing protocol

Each backend runs in its own serial process. JAX runtime is initialized once. Each explicit JIT workload is lowered and compiled with dynamic geometry/sample inputs; caches are cleared between workloads. “Cold total” is lowering + compilation + first execution, excluding process/import startup and fixture/reference setup. Host APIs include their internal compilation in first call and have no separate compiler measurement.

Numba uses a fresh isolated disk cache per run, but shares compiled helpers across workloads. Its first-call timings are therefore order-dependent. Warm timings use 5 repetitions. They are descriptive local measurements, not CI thresholds. Break-even estimates compare these calls only; preparation and process startup are excluded.

Accuracy & scope

Independent SciPy B-splines and rational quotient recurrences check values and derivatives. Numerical integration and multistart projection references use tighter settings. Projection compares squared distance and parameter bounds; equal-distance solutions may have different parameters. Expanded rows disclose absolute/relative tolerances and scaled error (≤1 passes).

Native default length algorithms differ. Surface area is a derived Gauss integration workflow. Prepared tables and inverse maps are JAX-only. Undefined frames on collapsed geometry are classified explicitly. Plot checks render and compare artist counts, not pixel images. This is broad deterministic coverage, not an exhaustive proof over every possible geometry.

Coverage & reproducibility

Includes basis derivatives through fourth order; polynomial, rational, planar and repeated-knot curves; Frenet frames, speed, curvature and torsion; surface partials, normals, mean/Gaussian curvature and area; curve/surface projection; isocurves, joining, approximate offsets, circular arcs and all five surface generators; energy/integration workflows; AD versus finite differences; prepared tables and inverse maps; plotting smoke checks and degenerate geometry.

Low-level coordinate/derivative helpers are exercised through their public geometric wrappers. Plot styling, low-level helper variants, every invalid-input policy and every optimizer configuration are not separately timed. See methodology, findings, coverage inventory. Recorded environment details are embedded below. Raw measurements, manifests and JUnit files are retained locally under outputs/results/ and excluded from Git.
